QUESTION: The Monetary Policy Committee (MPC) of the RBI, in its 62nd meeting held from August 3 to 5, 2026, voted unanimously to keep the policy repo rate under the Liquidity Adjustment Facility (LAF) at which level?
A. 5.00%
B. 5.25%
C. 5.50%
D. 5.75%
E. 4.90%
ANSWER: B
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: B – 5.25%</b>

The MPC voted <b>unanimously</b> to keep the repo rate unchanged for the <mark>fourth consecutive time</mark>, continuing its wait-and-watch approach.

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>The 62nd MPC meeting was held from <b>August 3 to 5, 2026</b>; the decision was announced on <b>August 5, 2026</b>.</li><li>Repo Rate – <b>5.25%</b>; Reverse Repo Rate – <b>3.35%</b>.</li><li>SDF – <b>5.00%</b>; MSF – <b>5.50%</b>; Bank Rate – <b>5.50%</b>.</li><li>CRR – <b>3.00%</b>; SLR – <b>18.00%</b>.</li><li>The MPC also decided to continue with the <b>neutral stance</b>.</li><li>Repo rate has remained unchanged at 5.25% since <b>February 2026</b>.</li></ul>
TAG: Repo Rate, RBI monetary policy decisions/Oneliner

QUESTION: Which country has been mentioned as the most recent (11th) country where UPI is operational, as per the PIB update dated 24 August 2026?
A. Greece
B. Cambodia
C. Qatar
D. Uzbekistan
E. Mauritius
ANSWER: D
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: D – Uzbekistan</b>

As per the PIB update dated <b>24 August 2026</b>, UPI is operational in <b>11 foreign countries</b> and <b>Uzbekistan</b> is the latest addition.

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>The 11 countries: <b>UAE, France, Bhutan, Sri Lanka, Nepal, Singapore, Mauritius, Qatar, Cambodia, Greece, Uzbekistan</b>.</li><li>Memory trick given in the source: <mark>Chan BF SANG MUST</mark> (Cambodia, Bhutan, France, Singapore, Sri Lanka, Nepal, UAE, Greece, Mauritius, Uzbekistan, Qatar).</li><li>UPI is operated by <b>NPCI</b>.</li></ul>
TAG: UPI international linkage, Cross-border digital payments/Oneliner

QUESTION: As per the RBI Monetary Policy Statement of August 2026, what is the rate of the Standing Deposit Facility (SDF)?
A. 5.00%
B. 5.25%
C. 5.50%
D. 3.35%
E. 3.00%
ANSWER: A
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: A – 5.00%</b>

The SDF rate is <b>25 basis points below the repo rate</b> (5.25%), while the MSF rate and Bank Rate are <b>25 bps above</b> it at 5.50%.

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>Repo – 5.25% | Reverse Repo – 3.35%</li><li>SDF – <b>5.00%</b> | MSF – 5.50% | Bank Rate – 5.50%</li><li>CRR – 3.00% | SLR – 18.00%</li><li>MPC decision on <b>August 5, 2026</b> was unanimous; stance remains <b>neutral</b>.</li></ul>
TAG: Standing Deposit Facility (SDF) Rate, Marginal Standing Facility (MSF) Rate/Number Based

QUESTION: Real-time cross-border payment services between India and the Maldives were launched by integrating India's UPI with which Maldivian payment system?
A. Rufiyaa Pay
B. Dhiraagu Pay
C. Maldives Pay Link
D. Fonaadhoo
E. Favara
ANSWER: E
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: E – Favara</b>

The integration of the <b>Maldives Instant Payment System Favara</b> with India's <b>UPI</b> was announced by the <b>Maldives Monetary Authority (MMA)</b>.

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>It strengthens cross-border digital financial connectivity and bilateral economic cooperation between India and the Maldives.</li><li><b>Payment limit:</b> up to <mark>USD 250 equivalent per month</mark> for eligible cross-border P2P transfers from <b>Maldives → India</b>.</li><li>Under <b>LRS</b> a resident individual in India may remit up to <b>USD 2,50,000</b> per financial year (outward remittance).</li></ul>

QUESTION: The RBI has postponed the implementation of the revised Basel III Pillar III Disclosure Framework by 6 months. What is the new implementation date?
A. 1 October 2026
B. 1 January 2027
C. 1 April 2027
D. 30 June 2027
E. 1 April 2028
ANSWER: C
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: C – 1 April 2027</b>

The RBI deferred the framework after stakeholders highlighted <b>implementation challenges</b>. Earlier it was proposed from the quarter ending <b>30 September 2026</b>.

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li><b>Objective of Pillar III:</b> promote market discipline through enhanced regulatory disclosures.</li><li>Banks will disclose: <b>risk-based capital ratios, leverage ratio, liquidity standards, risk management framework and RWA</b>.</li><li><b>First quarterly disclosure:</b> quarter ending 30 June 2027; thereafter every quarter.</li><li><b>First semi-annual disclosure:</b> half-year ending 30 September 2027.</li><li><b>First annual disclosure:</b> FY ending 31 March 2028.</li></ul>
TAG: Basel norms, Capital Adequacy Ratio/Oneliner

&#8203;
I. The MPC is a six-member committee with a 3 + 3 composition.
&#8203;
II. The inflation target is 4% with a tolerance band of -2/+2 till March 31, 2031.
&#8203;
III. The MPC meets at least four times a year and a minimum of four members are required for a meeting.
&#8203;
IV. The inflation target is reviewed and reset every year.

A. Only I and II
B. Only II and III
C. Only I and IV
D. All of I, II, III and IV
E. Only I, II and III
ANSWER: E
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: E – Only I, II and III</b>

<b>🔎 Statement-wise analysis</b>
<table><tr><th>Statement</th><th>Verdict</th><th>Explanation</th></tr><tr><td><b>I</b></td><td>✅ True</td><td>Six-member MPC {3 + 3}.</td></tr><tr><td><b>II</b></td><td>✅ True</td><td>4% with -2/+2 tolerance till 31 March 2031.</td></tr><tr><td><b>III</b></td><td>✅ True</td><td>At least <b>four meetings</b> a year; minimum <b>four members</b> needed.</td></tr><tr><td><b>IV</b></td><td>❌ False</td><td>The inflation target is set <b>once in five years</b>, not every year.</td></tr></table>

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>

QUESTION: A merchant with annual turnover above Rs.20 lakh receives a payment of Rs.2,00,000 through a debit card other than RuPay. What is the maximum MDR the merchant can be charged as per the RBI cap?
A. Rs.900
B. Rs.1,800
C. Rs.2,000
D. Rs.1,000
E. Rs.450
ANSWER: D
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: D – Rs.1,000</b>

MDR on debit cards other than RuPay for businesses with annual turnover of Rs.20 lakh or more is capped at <b>0.9% or Rs.1,000, whichever is lower</b>.

<b>🧮 Calculation</b>
<ul><li>0.9% of Rs.2,00,000 = <b>Rs.1,800</b></li><li>Cap = Rs.1,000 → the <b>lower</b> value applies = <mark>Rs.1,000</mark>.</li></ul>

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>Example in the source: Rs.10,000 × 0.90% = <b>Rs.90</b>.</li><li>UPI and UPI QR – currently <b>zero MDR</b>; RuPay debit card – zero MDR under prescribed electronic-mode provisions.</li><li>Small merchant = turnover up to <b>Rs.20 lakh</b> in the previous FY.</li><li>As of 24 August 2026 merchants are not yet being charged MDR on UPI.</li></ul>
TAG: UPI, Payment aggregators and gateways/Number Based

QUESTION: Which of the following payment modes currently has Zero MDR for merchants as per the information given?
A. Credit Card
B. Other Debit Cards – Small Merchant
C. RuPay Debit Card
D. Other Debit Cards – Merchant with turnover above Rs.20 lakh
E. Credit card under merchant-acquirer agreement
ANSWER: C
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: C – RuPay Debit Card</b>

<b>📊 MDR applicability</b>
<table><tr><th>Payment mode</th><th>MDR?</th></tr><tr><td>UPI / BHIM-UPI</td><td>Zero MDR</td></tr><tr><td>UPI QR / BHIM-UPI QR</td><td>Zero MDR</td></tr><tr><td><b>RuPay Debit Card</b></td><td>Zero MDR (prescribed electronic-mode provisions)</td></tr><tr><td>Other debit cards – small merchant (turnover up to Rs.20 lakh)</td><td>MDR can apply</td></tr><tr><td>Other debit cards – other merchant (above Rs.20 lakh)</td><td>MDR can apply</td></tr><tr><td>Credit card</td><td>MDR generally applies as per merchant/acquirer agreement</td></tr></table>

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>MDR = fee that merchants pay to banks and payment service providers to process digital payments.</li><li>The Government clarified on <b>8 August 2026</b> that consumers and P2P transactions will remain free; any future UPI MDR would apply only to a limited category of merchant transactions above a threshold.</li></ul>
TAG: UPI, RuPay/Banking Terminology

QUESTION: The revised RBI loan recovery rules are scheduled to come into effect from which date?
A. 1 January 2027
B. 1 October 2026
C. 1 April 2027
D. 1 July 2026
E. 31 March 2027
ANSWER: A
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: A – 1 January 2027</b>

The earlier proposed implementation date was <b>1 October 2026</b>; the revised effective date is <mark>1 January 2027</mark>.

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>Applicable to <b>Commercial Banks, NBFCs, Co-operative Banks and Housing Finance Companies (HFCs)</b>.</li><li>Recovery agents / bank employees can normally contact or visit a borrower or guarantor only between <b>8:00 am and 7:00 pm</b>.</li><li>Banks must maintain an <b>up-to-date list of recovery agencies on their website</b>.</li><li>Borrower/guarantor must be informed about the recovery agency <b>at least one day before the first in-person visit</b>; a change of agency must also be notified.</li></ul>
TAG: RBI master directions and circulars, NBFC regulations/Oneliner

QUESTION: What is 'Bank in a Box', which was launched by Union Home Minister and Minister of Cooperation Amit Shah?
A. A mobile ATM van for rural areas
B. A scheme providing cash deposit kiosks for post offices
C. An integrated technology and service package for Urban Cooperative Banks
D. A digital lending app for farmers
E. A security box facility for bank lockers
ANSWER: C
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: C – An integrated technology and service package for Urban Cooperative Banks (UCBs)</b>

'Bank in a Box' does <b>not literally mean a bank inside a box</b>.

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>

QUESTION: What is the permissible deposit period for an FCNR(B) deposit?
A. Minimum 1 year – Maximum 5 years
B. Minimum 6 months – Maximum 3 years
C. Minimum 1 year – Maximum 10 years
D. Minimum 7 days – Maximum 5 years
E. Minimum 2 years – Maximum 5 years
ANSWER: A
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: A – Minimum 1 year to Maximum 5 years</b>

<b>📌 Key pointers on FCNR(B)</b>
<ul><li><b>Full form:</b> Foreign Currency Non-Resident (Bank) Account.</li><li>Only a <b>Term/Fixed Deposit</b>; <b>no</b> savings/current account and <b>no</b> recurring deposit.</li><li>For NRIs / eligible non-residents; held in <b>permitted freely convertible foreign currencies</b>.</li><li>Principal and interest are <b>repatriable</b>; very low / no INR exchange-rate risk to the depositor.</li><li>Interest rate is decided by the bank subject to RBI rules/ceilings; regulator – <b>RBI</b>.</li><li>In the news because the RBI's <mark>special forex-swap facility</mark> attracted exceptionally large foreign-currency inflows.</li></ul>
TAG: Foreign Portfolio Investment, Banking Structure, Regulation & Reforms/Oneliner

QUESTION: Public Sector Banks have proposed a separate sub-target of how much for climate and transition finance within the existing 40% Priority Sector Lending (PSL) requirement?
A. 5%
B. 2%
C. 1%
D. 10%
E. 4%
ANSWER: B
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: B – 2%</b>

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>The proposed <b>2%</b> sub-target would be <b>carved out of</b> the existing <b>40% of Adjusted Net Bank Credit (ANBC)</b> PSL requirement for Scheduled Commercial Banks.</li><li>Currently there is <b>no separate sub-target</b> for climate/transition finance under PSL.</li><li>Existing sub-targets include <b>agriculture – 18%</b> and <b>micro enterprises – 7.5%</b>.</li></ul>
TAG: Priority Sector Lending, Public Sector Banks/Number Based

QUESTION: The RBI reduced the Priority Sector Lending (PSL) target for Small Finance Banks (SFBs) from 75% to what percentage of ANBC or CEOBE, whichever is higher, effective FY 2025-26?
A. 50%
B. 65%
C. 60%
D. 40%
E. 55%
ANSWER: C
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: C – 60%</b>

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>The 60% target is split: <b>40% for core PSL sub-sectors</b> (agriculture, MSMEs, housing) and <b>20% for other PSL sub-sectors</b> based on the bank's competitive edge.</li><li>Revised PSL guidelines came into force on <b>1 April 2025</b>, replacing the 2020 directions.</li><li>Targets: <b>Commercial banks</b> (domestic and foreign with ≥ 20 branches) – <b>40%</b>; <b>RRBs</b> – <b>75%</b>; <b>SFBs and UCBs</b> – <b>60%</b>.</li><li><b>CEOBE</b> = Credit Equivalent of Off-Balance Sheet Exposures.</li></ul>
TAG: Priority Sector Lending, Small Finance Banks/Number Based

A. Bank of India
B. Central Bank of India
C. Punjab National Bank
D. Bank of Baroda
E. State Bank of India
ANSWER: E

QUESTION: Under RBI's Scale Based Regulation, a qualifying NBFC with a standalone asset size of at least how much is automatically placed in the Upper Layer (NBFC-UL)?
A. Rs.1,000 crore
B. Rs.10,000 crore
C. Rs.50,000 crore
D. Rs.5 lakh crore
E. Rs.1 lakh crore
ANSWER: E
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: E – Rs.1 lakh crore (Rs.1,00,000 crore)</b>

<b>📊 NBFC layers under SBR</b>
<table><tr><th>Layer</th><th>Criterion</th></tr><tr><td>Base Layer (NBFC-BL)</td><td>Non-deposit-taking NBFCs with assets below Rs.1,000 crore</td></tr><tr><td>Middle Layer (NBFC-ML)</td><td>Non-deposit-taking NBFCs with assets of Rs.1,000 crore and above</td></tr><tr><td>Upper Layer (NBFC-UL)</td><td>Standalone assets of Rs.1 lakh crore or more</td></tr><tr><td>Top Layer (NBFC-TL)</td><td>Structurally designed to remain empty under normal conditions</td></tr></table>

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>RBI released the list on <b>6 August 2026</b> identifying <b>17 NBFC-UL</b> entities for FY 2026-27.</li><li>Major names: REC, PFC, IRFC, Bajaj Finance, Shriram Finance, LIC Housing Finance, Tata Capital and Muthoot Finance.</li><li>Once classified as NBFC-UL, an NBFC remains under enhanced regulation for <b>at least 5 years</b>.</li></ul>
TAG: NBFC regulations, RBI master directions and circulars/Number Based

QUESTION: Which layer of the RBI's Scale Based Regulation for NBFCs is structurally designed to remain empty under normal economic conditions?
A. Top Layer
B. Upper Layer
C. Middle Layer
D. Base Layer
E. Special Layer
ANSWER: A
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: A – Top Layer (NBFC-TL)</b>

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>Order of layers: <b>Base → Middle → Upper → Top</b>.</li><li>NBFCs are classified based on <b>size, activity and perceived riskiness</b>.</li><li>RBI identified <b>17 NBFC-UL</b> entities for FY 2026-27 (list released 6 August 2026) and they stay in the layer for at least <b>5 years</b>.</li></ul>

Compensation per day of delay by the bank</td><td>c. Rs.100</td></tr><tr><td>4. Frequent daily limit in the cooling period for a new UPI ID/device</td><td>d. Rs.5,000</td></tr></table>

&#8203;

&#8203;
II. Users can link up to 25 bank accounts per day per UPI app.
&#8203;
III. For a pending payment, the status can be checked only 3 times with a mandatory gap of 90 seconds.
&#8203;
IV. UPI IDs inactive for over 6 months are automatically disabled.
QUESTION: Which of the statements given above are correct?
A. Only I and II
B. Only I, II and III
C. Only II, III and IV
D. All of I, II, III and IV
E. Only I and IV
ANSWER: B
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: B – Only I, II and III</b>

<b>🔎 Statement-wise analysis</b>
<table><tr><th>Statement</th><th>Verdict</th><th>Explanation</th></tr><tr><td><b>I</b></td><td>✅ True</td><td>Balance check cap: <b>50 times per day per app</b>; exceeding blocks balance checks for 24 hours.</td></tr><tr><td><b>II</b></td><td>✅ True</td><td>Up to <b>25 bank accounts per day</b> per UPI app.</td></tr><tr><td><b>III</b></td><td>✅ True</td><td>Pending status can be checked <b>3 times</b> with a <b>90-second</b> gap.</td></tr><tr><td><b>IV</b></td><td>❌ False</td><td>UPI IDs inactive for over <b>12 months</b> (not 6) are automatically disabled.</td></tr></table>

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>

ANSWER: A
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: A – 2008</b>

<b>📌 Key pointers on NPCI</b>
<ul><li>Manages <b>retail payments and settlement systems</b> in India; established by the <b>RBI and IBA</b>.</li><li>Goal: make India a digital economy through secure, low-cost payment solutions.</li><li>Products: <b>RuPay, UPI, BHIM, NACH, Foreign Inward Remittance (FIR), Bharat QR, UPI Lite, UPI 123 Pay, UPI One World, UPI Circle, AePS</b>.</li><li><b>UPI milestones:</b> pilot/soft launch on <b>11 April 2016</b>; customer go-live on <b>25 August 2016</b> with <b>21 banks</b>.</li><li>FY 2025-26: over <b>24,162 crore</b> transactions worth about <b>Rs.314 lakh crore</b>; about <b>66 crore</b> transactions per day; <b>700+</b> banks; roughly <b>85%</b> of India's digital-payment transactions.</li></ul>
TAG: NPCI, NPCI products and initiatives/Static GK

QUESTION: Which NPCI product is described as a QR code specification that allows users to make mobile payments to merchants?
A. BHIM
B. NACH
C. Bharat QR
D. RuPay
E. AePS
ANSWER: C
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: C – Bharat QR (Quick Response)</b>

<b>📌 NPCI products as described in the source</b>
<table><tr><th>Product</th><th>Description</th></tr><tr><td>RuPay</td><td>Offers credit, debit and international cards</td></tr><tr><td>UPI</td><td>Immediate money transfers, multiple bank accounts, QR payments</td></tr><tr><td>BHIM</td><td>Real-time payment interface using Aadhaar number or VPA</td></tr><tr><td>NACH</td><td>Web-based solution for electronic transactions between banks, FIs, corporations and the government</td></tr><tr><td>FIR</td><td>Allows individuals in India to receive foreign funds directly into UPI-linked bank accounts</td></tr><tr><td><b>Bharat QR</b></td><td>QR code specification developed by NPCI for merchant mobile payments</td></tr></table>

QUESTION: As per the general rule given in the source, an individual is treated as a Resident in India (and hence not an NRI) if he/she satisfies any one of which conditions?
A. Stay of 90 days or more in the year OR 365 days in preceding 4 years
B. Stay of 182 days or more in the year OR 60 days in the year and 365 days or more in the preceding 4 years
C. Stay of 182 days in the year AND 365 days in the preceding 4 years
D. Stay of 120 days in the year OR 60 days in the year and 365 days in the preceding 4 years
E. Stay of 60 days in the year OR 182 days in the preceding 4 years
ANSWER: B
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: B – 182 days or more; OR 60 days or more in the year AND 365 days or more in the immediately preceding 4 years</b>

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>If <b>any one</b> condition is satisfied → <b>Resident in India (not NRI)</b>.</li><li>If <b>none</b> is satisfied → <b>NRI</b>.</li><li><b>NRE</b> = Non-Resident External – foreign income kept in INR; <b>NRO</b> = Non-Resident Ordinary – India-earned income (rent, pension, dividends) in INR.</li><li><b>FCNR(B)</b> – foreign currency term deposit.</li></ul>
TAG: Foreign Portfolio Investment, Economy, Credit & Financial Sector Updates/Banking Terminology

QUESTION: Which of the following correctly describes an NRO (Non-Resident Ordinary) account?
A. A foreign-currency term deposit held by NRIs
B. An account in INR to keep foreign income, with fully repatriable principal and interest
C. An account that is held only in US dollars
D. An INR account used to manage income earned in India, such as rent, pension and dividends
E. A recurring deposit account in foreign currency
ANSWER: D
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: D – An INR account used to manage income earned in India, such as rent, pension and dividends</b>

<b>📊 Easy difference</b>
<table><tr><th>Account</th><th>Purpose</th><th>Repatriation</th></tr><tr><td>NRE</td><td>Foreign income → INR account</td><td>Principal and interest generally fully repatriable</td></tr><tr><td>NRO</td><td>Indian income → INR account</td><td>Subject to applicable rules and limits</td></tr><tr><td>FCNR(B)</td><td>Term deposit in foreign currency</td><td>Principal and interest repatriable</td></tr></table>
TAG: Foreign Portfolio Investment, Banking Structure, Regulation & Reforms/Banking Terminology

QUESTION: Under the Liberalised Remittance Scheme (LRS), a resident individual may remit up to how much in one financial year (April–March)?
A. USD 1,00,000
B. USD 2,50,000
C. USD 5,00,000
D. USD 25,000
E. USD 10,00,000
ANSWER: B
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: B – USD 2,50,000</b>

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>LRS is mainly an <b>outward remittance scheme</b> for resident individuals in India.</li><li>The limit is an <b>overall combined ceiling</b>, not a separate USD 2,50,000 limit for each purpose.</li><li>Foreign country → India: <b>no general LRS limit</b>.</li></ul>

QUESTION: As per Basel III international norms, banks must maintain a minimum Capital Adequacy Ratio (Total Capital Ratio) of what percentage of Risk-Weighted Assets (RWA)?
A. 4%
B. 6%
C. 12%
D. 8%
E. 9%
ANSWER: D
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: D – 8% of RWA</b>

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>Basel norms are international banking rules issued by the <b>Basel Committee on Banking Supervision (BCBS)</b>, based at the <b>Bank for International Settlements (BIS), Basel, Switzerland</b>.</li><li>Evolution: <b>Basel I (1988) → Basel II (2004) → Basel III (2010)</b>.</li><li>Pillar III disclosure framework implementation postponed by the RBI to <b>1 April 2027</b>.</li></ul>
TAG: Basel norms, Capital Adequacy Ratio/Number Based

TEXT: <b>Match the pillars of Basel III in List-I with their key idea in List-II:</b><br><table><tr><th>List-I</th><th>List-II</th></tr><tr><td>1. Pillar 1</td><td>a. Market discipline / Transparency and disclosure</td></tr><tr><td>2. Pillar 2</td><td>b. Minimum capital requirements / Adequate capital</td></tr><tr><td>3. Pillar 3</td><td>c. Supervisory review process / Regulatory supervision</td></tr></table>
QUESTION: Choose the option that gives the correct match.
A. 1-a, 2-b, 3-c
B. 1-c, 2-b, 3-a
C. 1-b, 2-c, 3-a
D. 1-b, 2-a, 3-c
E. 1-c, 2-a, 3-b
ANSWER: C
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: C – 1-b, 2-c, 3-a</b>

<b>📊 Basel III – three pillars</b>
<table><tr><th>Pillar</th><th>Focus</th><th>Key idea</th></tr><tr><td>Pillar 1</td><td>Minimum capital against credit, market and operational risk</td><td>Adequate Capital</td></tr><tr><td>Pillar 2</td><td>Supervisors assess capital adequacy, risk management and internal controls</td><td>Regulatory Supervision</td></tr><tr><td>Pillar 3</td><td>Banks disclose capital, risks, exposures and risk-management practices</td><td>Transparency and Disclosure</td></tr></table>
TAG: Basel norms, Capital Adequacy Ratio/Match the Column

QUESTION: The Basel Committee on Banking Supervision (BCBS), which issues Basel norms, is based at the Bank for International Settlements (BIS) in which city?
A. Geneva
B. Basel
C. Zurich
D. Frankfurt
E. Paris
ANSWER: B
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: B – Basel, Switzerland</b>

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>Basel norms are international banking rules designed to make banks <b>safer and financially stronger</b>.</li><li>Basel I – <b>1988</b>; Basel II – <b>2004</b>; Basel III – <b>2010</b>.</li><li>Minimum Total Capital Ratio under Basel III: <b>8% of RWA</b>.</li></ul>
TAG: Basel norms, Financial institutions and headquarters/Static GK

QUESTION: Which of the following types of banks can accept deposits and provide payment/remittance facilities but cannot undertake normal lending like a regular commercial bank?
A. Payments Banks
B. Small Finance Banks
C. Regional Rural Banks
D. Foreign Banks operating in India
E. Public Sector Banks
ANSWER: A
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: A – Payments Banks</b>

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>Payments Banks are <b>differentiated banks</b>.</li><li>A <b>commercial bank</b> accepts public deposits and provides loans/credit; income comes mainly from the lending-deposit rate difference and banking fees.</li><li>Types of commercial banks: <b>Public Sector Banks, Private Sector Banks, Foreign Banks in India, Regional Rural Banks and Small Finance Banks</b>.</li></ul>

QUESTION: What does CEOBE stand for in the context of Priority Sector Lending targets?
A. Credit Equivalent of Off-Balance Sheet Exposures
B. Capital Equity of Overall Banking Exposure
C. Credit Entitlement of Overdue Bank Exposures
D. Cumulative Exposure of Outstanding Bank Equity
E. Central Equivalent of Official Bank Exposures
ANSWER: A
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: A – Credit Equivalent of Off-Balance Sheet Exposures</b>

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>CEOBE is a measure used to determine a bank's commitment to lending to priority sectors.</li><li>PSL targets are expressed as a percentage of <b>ANBC (Adjusted Net Bank Credit)</b> or <b>CEOBE, whichever is higher</b>.</li><li>Revised PSL guidelines effective <b>1 April 2025</b> apply to commercial banks, RRBs, SFBs, Local Area Banks and Primary (Urban) Co-operative Banks.</li></ul>

QUESTION: The proposed amendment to which Act gives the Central Government legal flexibility to decide in future whether MDR should be charged on certain UPI and RuPay debit card transactions?
A. Reserve Bank of India Act, 1934
B. Banking Regulation Act, 1949
C. Payment and Settlement Systems Act, 2007
D. Information Technology Act, 2000
E. Negotiable Instruments Act, 1881
ANSWER: C
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: C – Payment and Settlement Systems (PSS) Act, 2007</b>

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>The amendment does <b>not immediately impose</b> charges on UPI transactions; no instant fee has been announced.</li><li>It may apply particularly to <b>large merchants</b> and payments above a threshold (reports mention about <b>Rs.2,000</b>).</li><li>Times of India (Aug 5, 2026) reported a likely levy of <b>0.25% to 0.4%</b> MDR on UPI payments above Rs.2,000 to businesses, while person-to-person payments remain free.</li><li>Since <b>January 2020</b>, almost no MDR has been charged on UPI and RuPay debit card transactions, so banks and PSPs have borne most of the costs.</li><li>P2P transfers are expected to remain free; no final decision has been announced.</li></ul>
TAG: UPI, RuPay/Oneliner

QUESTION: As per the UPI transaction charges mentioned in the source, an interchange fee of 0.5% – 1.1% applies to merchant transactions above Rs.2,000 made via which mode, and who bears it?
A. Bank-to-bank UPI transfers; the customer
B. Wallets / Prepaid Payment Instruments; the merchant
C. RuPay debit cards; the customer
D. UPI Lite; the bank
E. UPI 123Pay; the customer
ANSWER: B
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: B – Wallets / Prepaid Payment Instruments (PPI); borne by the merchant</b>

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li><b>Individual users:</b> bank-to-bank UPI transfers remain <b>completely free</b> for personal use.</li><li><b>Merchants (PPI/Wallets):</b> interchange fee of <b>0.5% – 1.1%</b> on transactions above <b>Rs.2,000</b>; paid by the <b>merchant</b>, not the customer.</li><li><b>Credit on UPI:</b> RuPay credit cards or pre-sanctioned credit lines may attract MDR of roughly <b>1.1% to 2%</b>, borne by the merchant.</li></ul>

QUESTION: Which of the following is NOT mentioned as an example of an All India Financial Institution (AIFI) in the source?

D. National Housing Bank (NHB)
E. SIDBI
ANSWER: B
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: B – SEBI</b>

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>A <b>Financial Institution (FI)</b> is an umbrella term for a company dealing with financial and monetary transactions, including loans, deposits and/or investments.</li><li><b>AIFIs</b> are regulated and supervised by the <b>Reserve Bank of India</b>.</li><li>Examples: <b>NaBFID, EXIM Bank, NABARD, NHB, SIDBI</b>.</li><li>SEBI is the regulator of securities and commodity markets, not an AIFI.</li></ul>

QUESTION: India became a party to the Ramsar Convention in which year?
A. 1971
B. 1975
C. 1981
D. 1982
E. 1990
ANSWER: D
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: D – 1982 (1 February 1982)</b>

<b>📌 Ramsar Convention – Convention on Wetlands</b>
<ul><li>Meeting held on <b>2 February 1971</b> at <b>Ramsar, Iran</b>; came into force in <b>1975</b>.</li><li><b>World Wetlands Day</b> – 2 February.</li><li>India's first two Ramsar Sites (designated 1981): <b>Chilika Lake (Odisha)</b> and <b>Keoladeo National Park (Rajasthan)</b>.</li></ul>
TAG: Ramsar Sites & Wetlands, Ramsar Convention and secretariat/Number Based

QUESTION: What is the Fair and Remunerative Price (FRP) of sugarcane for the 2026-27 sugar season (starting 1 October 2026)?
A. Rs.355 per quintal
B. Rs.365 per quintal
C. Rs.375 per quintal
D. Rs.338.3 per quintal
E. Rs.385 per quintal
ANSWER: B
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: B – Rs.365 per quintal</b>

<b>📌 Key pointers</b>
<ul><li>Applies to a <b>basic sugar recovery rate of 10.25%</b>.</li><li>FRP for 2025-26 was <b>Rs.355</b>; a hike of <b>Rs.10 per quintal</b> was approved for 2026-27.</li><li>Farmers get an extra <b>Rs.3.56 per quintal</b> for every 0.1% increase in recovery above 10.25%.</li><li>No deduction if recovery falls below <b>9.5%</b>; minimum guaranteed <b>Rs.338.3 per quintal</b>.</li></ul>
TAG: MSP and procurement decisions, Agriculture and farmers' schemes/Number Based

TEXT: <b>Consider the following statements about the sugarcane FRP for 2026-27:</b>
&#8203;
I. The FRP for 2025-26 was Rs.355 per quintal.
&#8203;
II. A hike of Rs.10 per quintal was approved for 2026-27.
&#8203;
III. Farmers receive an extra Rs.3.56 per quintal for every 0.1% increase in recovery above 10.25%.
&#8203;
IV. The basic sugar recovery rate for FRP is 9.5%.
QUESTION: Which of the statements given above are correct?
A. Only I and II
B. Only II and IV
C. Only I and IV
D. Only I, II and III
E. All of I, II, III and IV
ANSWER: D
SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: D – Only I, II and III</b>

<b>🔎 Statement-wise analysis</b>
<table><tr><th>Statement</th><th>Verdict</th><th>Explanation</th></tr><tr><td><b>I</b></td><td>✅ True</td><td>Current rate (2025-26): Rs.355.</td></tr><tr><td><b>II</b></td><td>✅ True</td><td>Rs.355 + Rs.10 = <b>Rs.365</b>.</td></tr><tr><td><b>III</b></td><td>✅ True</td><td>Premium of Rs.3.56 per 0.1% above 10.25%.</td></tr><tr><td><b>IV</b></td><td>❌ False</td><td>The basic recovery rate is <b>10.25%</b>; 9.5% is the safety floor below which no deduction is made.</td></tr></table>

B. Rs.7,500 per month
C. Rs.10,000 per month
D. Rs.9,000 per month

B. Rs.75,000 crore
C. Rs.1 trillion
D. Rs.2 trillion
E. None of these

SOLUTION: <b>✅ Correct answer: C – Rs.1 trillion (Rs.1 lakh crore)</b>
